# StruKit — Build Log

GitHub: ThatcherEngineering/StruKit (private). Branch `main` is the source, pushed after every build. Branch `release` is the ready-to-run Windows folder that the installer and the automatic update download. Rulings are in `STRUKIT-RULINGS.md` (current through 2026-11-26 10:35).

**Installed on Zach's laptop 2026-11-21 09:33** (`OneDrive\Documents\strukit`, by `Install-StruKit.bat`). The bridge runs it (`run_tool('strukit', ['--version'])`). It updates itself from `release` every 2 hours; `C:\ZET-G\StruKit\StruKit-Get-Latest.bat` updates at once.

**Where everything lives**
- Code, tests, stored job projects (`tests/data`) and their builders (`jobs/`): GitHub `ThatcherEngineering/StruKit`.
- Rules for every session: `CLAUDE.md` in the repo. Rulings: `docs/STRUKIT-RULINGS.md`. This log: `docs/STRUKIT-BUILD-LOG.md`. Which reference file is which, and the pages used: `docs/REFERENCES.md`. The rulings and this log are also in the Project as `claude/STRUKIT-RULINGS.md` and `claude/STRUKIT-BUILD-LOG.md`; keep both in step.
- Other StruKit docs in the Project: `claude/STRUKIT-PHASE1-PROPOSAL-2026-11-20.md`, `claude/STRUKIT-JURIS-FINDINGS-2026-11-20.md` (section 3 items still open), `claude/STRUKIT-INPUT-SPEC.md` and `claude/fixtures/strukit/` (on hold until Zach restarts the ProgeCAD side), `claude/STRUKIT-TO-PROGECAD-*` and `claude/PROGECAD-TO-STRUKIT-*`.
- Reference standards: Zach's folder (see `docs/REFERENCES.md`). Folder access to it and to `C:\ZET-G\ProgeCAD\_handoff` was granted to the scheduled session on 2026-11-26. The 14th Ed. Manual scan (300 dpi, OCR works) is sharper than the 15th Ed. scan for Part 10 tables.
- Releasing: `tools/release.sh` runs the suite twice (about 5 min each); from a session with a 10-minute command limit run it in the background (`nohup ... &`) and wait for "release branch now at build"; do not edit files until it ends (the release copies the working tree).
- A scheduled session starts each weekday at 5:10 AM Denver time, reads these files and sends Zach one check-in with the Next list; it builds nothing until he says go. Before the session ends it updates this section and the Project copies.

## Zach's reference folder (2026-11-23 07:39)
`C:\Users\zacht\OneDrive\Desktop\StrucutralDesign` (spelled that way; inside the connected Desktop folder, so any chat can list and stage it). Contents on 2026-11-23:
- Already in use: `A360-22W-ewr.pdf` (AISC 360-22), `P901-23W.pdf` and `P902-23W.pdf` (AISC Design Examples v16.0, Vol. 1 and 2), `SHAPE160.xlsx`, `ACI-318-19.pdf`, `885219892-ASCE-SE-7-22.pdf` (ASCE 7-22, page images, OCR needed), `Steel-Structures2024-[www.konkur.in].pdf` (AISC Design Guide 1, 3rd Ed.).
- **New, staged and identified, not yet used:**
  - `721340051-Special-Design-Provisions-...-2021.pdf`: **SDPWS 2021 with commentary**, 151 pages, has text (two columns, interleaved in the extract). Opens wood shear walls and diaphragms.
  - `715304102-TMS-402-602-16-Masonry-Code.pdf`: **TMS 402/602-16**, 383 pages, page images (OCR needed). Opens CMU walls. Every page is stamped "Licensed to" another person's name. 2016 is the edition the 2018 and 2021 IBC reference; confirm against the governing code before building.
  - `318966905-Shearwall-Design-Examples.pdf`: AWC presentation "Shearwall Basics Using SDPWS" (2015, 132 slides, written to the 2008 SDPWS). Worked shear wall examples; usable as an independent check where the provisions did not change.
  - `471022586-Lateral-Design-of-Mid-Rise-Wood-Structures.pdf`: WoodWorks presentation (2016, 78 slides). Background on load path and multi-story lateral design; no provisions.
- **Added by Zach 2026-11-23 16:36, staged and read (all five open normally and have text; text copies in the StruKit chat workspace under `refs/new5`):**
  - `526025875-Wind-Design-Manual-Based-on-the-2018-IBC-and-ASCE-7-16.pdf`: SEAOC/ICC **Wind Design Manual** (355 pages, scanned with OCR text). 16 worked examples. Examples 8A and 8B (three-story building, main system by Ch. 27 and cladding by Ch. 30) are now StruKit's published wind reference (0.120.0). Written to ASCE 7-16: Kd sits in the velocity pressure and Kz uses the older constants, so pressures match ASCE 7-22 to about 0.6 percent, coefficients exactly. Other examples of use later: 1 (enclosure classification), 2 (topographic factor Kzt), 4 (gust factor), 7A/7B (simple diaphragm building), 9 (14-story), 10 to 16 (rooftop solar, carports).
  - `719353002-Steel-Design-Guide-40-Rain-Loads-and-Ponding.pdf`: **AISC Design Guide 40** (70 pages). Rain load and ponding; Examples 1 and 2 are the reference for the Rain Load screen (0.120.0). Examples 4 and 5 (free-draining bays, Sect. 8.3 slope check) not used yet.
  - `719353933-AISC-Design-Guide-24-Hollow-Structural-Section-Connections-2nd-Edition.pdf`: **AISC Design Guide 24, 2nd Ed.** (356 pages). HSS connections: not used yet.
  - `683124131-Steel-Design-Guide-21-Welded-Connections-...-2nd-Edition.pdf`: **AISC Design Guide 21, 2nd Ed.** (334 pages). Welded connections: not used yet.
  - `674562233-34-AISC-Design-Guide-34-Steel-Framed-Stairway-Design-2018.pdf`: **AISC Design Guide 34** (114 pages). Steel stairs: not used yet.
- The 2018 NDS and its Supplement were uploaded to the chat but are not in this folder.
- Most of these files carry file-sharing-site or third-party licence marks and are probably not publisher copies. Zach was told on 2026-11-23; his call.

## Built without its governing document on file
1. Centred base plate thickness: Manual Part 14 equations taken from Design Example J.6. (Design Guide 1 Sect. 4.3.1 covers the same method; not yet re-cited.)
2. Footing with moment: soil pressure by statics, then ACI 318-19 for the concrete.
3. The beam and frame solver is textbook stiffness analysis, validated against AISC's benchmark problems.

## Builds
- 0.1.0 to 0.19.0: run contract; steel beams, columns, frames; footings; welds and bolts; centred base plate; footing with moment; worksheets, calc sheets, package cover; the StruKit window, installer and updater.
- 0.20.0 / 0.21.0: column base plate with moment; rod anchorage in concrete (ACI 318-19 Chapter 17).
- 0.22.0 to 0.22.2 (2026-11-23): base plate with moment re-based on and tested against AISC Design Guide 1; rod data from its tables; lower breakout value for a row of rods; sheet notes factual only.
- 0.23.0: ASCE/SEI 7-22 basic load combinations fill the project's ASD and LRFD lists. Snow is strength-level in 7-22.
- 0.24.0: Roof Snow Load worksheet, ASCE/SEI 7-22 Chapter 7, validated against Commentary C7 Examples 1 and 3. Example 3's drift numbers in the commentary are internally inconsistent; use a loose tolerance when drifts are built and tell Zach.
- 0.25.0: Wood Beam worksheet, 2018 NDS: dimension lumber (Supplement Tables 1B, 4A; 34 species), one to six plies, simply supported. Reproduces Header A of job 2026-011.
- 0.26.0: Wood Post worksheet, NDS 3.6 and 3.7. Reproduces the Deck Column of job 2026-011.
- 0.27.0: Table 4D timbers for 29 species (read by `tools/parse_4d.py`, each group confirmed by its printed classification label; eight species and Southern Pine left out); posts with lateral load, NDS Eq. 3.9-3 and 3.9-4 (hand-calculation validation only).
- 0.28.0: wood beams take timbers 5x5 and larger (Table 4D by classification; size factor (12/d)^(1/9) above 12 in. deep). Hand-calculation test.
- **0.29.0 (2026-11-23): joists and rafters by spacing.** The Wood Beam screen has a member spacing and an "area" load type in psf; the load line on the sheet shows psf x spacing = plf. Reproduces the Deck Joist of job 2026-011 (2x10 at 16 in, 60 psf: bending 118.1 psi against 1,138.5, shear 20.2, bearing 41.6, deflection 0.005 in). 218 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Joist.pdf`.
- **0.30.0 (2026-11-23): wood shear walls.** New `shearwall.py`, sheet and screen "Wood Shear Wall": SDPWS-2021 Sect. 4.3, individual full-height segments in one line, wood structural panels blocked on one side. Table 4.3A (12 rows, read from the page image twice), specific gravity factor (note 3), 10d note 10, design factors of 4.1.4 (wind/seismic, ASD/LRFD), 3.5:1 limit, 2b/h for h/b over 2 with shear shared by capacity (4.3.5.5.1 exc. 1), chord T = C (Eq. 4.3-7), deflection (Eq. 4.3-1), dimensioned elevation. Validation: hand calculations and two relations from the AWC shear wall slides (no worked example in the 2021 edition on file yet; `318966905-Shearwall-Design-Examples.pdf` in the reference folder not yet read). Not built: unblocked, two-sided, perforated, force transfer, dead load against uplift. 226 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Shear-Wall.pdf`.
- **0.31.0 (2026-11-23): LVL beams.** New `scl.py`, `ws_wood.lvl_beam`, screen "LVL Beam": Versa-Lam, ICC-ES ESR-1040 Table 1 (38 grades, `tools/parse_esr1040.py`), 2018 NDS Ch. 8, joist orientation, 1 to 4 plies, dry use. CV = (12/d)^(1/9), lesser of CV and CL (NDS 8.3.6.1), Cr 1.04, Emin from apparent E (note 6), deflection = bending with true E + shear 19.2M/(Ebd) (note 11 in terms of moment). Validation: Boise design guide LVL-W2000 allowable moment and shear (3 sizes, exact); job 2026-011 Roof Beam A bending 2,240.2 / 2,803.3, shear 197.1, bearing 520.0 (exact). **StruCalc's deflection (0.341 in) is bending only; with the ESR shear term it is 0.382 in (12 percent more). Zach told.** 231 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-LVL-Beam.pdf`.
- 0.31.1 (2026-11-23 08:41, Zach: "make the LVLs look a touch different than wood and the glulams a touch different than LVLs. Just visually"): `memberart.wood_elevation` / `wood_section` take `kind` = sawn | lvl | glulam. Sawn: wavy grain, growth rings per ply. LVL: darker, fine dashes on the face, fine vertical veneer lines in section. Glulam: lighter, straight glue lines, laminations with rings in section. Pass `kind` in the picture dict.
- **0.32.0 (2026-11-23): LVL columns.** `ws_wood.lvl_post`, screen "LVL Column": one-piece Versa-Lam, CP with c = 0.9, eccentric axial load by NDS Eq. 15.4-3 / 15.4-4 (`scl.eccentric_column`), plank Fb about either axis (stated assumption, the lower value). Validation: Boise LVL-W2000 1.8E 2650 column tables, 7 entries within 0.4 percent when the guide's Emin (1.05 factor) is used; StruKit uses NDS Eq. D-4 (1.03) per ESR-1040 note 6, about 1 percent lower. Not built: lateral load on LVL columns, multi-ply columns (NDS 15.3). 233 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-LVL-Column.pdf`.
- **0.33.0 (2026-11-23): glulam beams.** `glulam.py`, `ws_wood.glulam_beam`, screen "Glulam Beam": Table 5A stress classes (7) plus 24F-V4 DF/DF from Table 5A Expanded; CV (Eq. 5.3-1, x = 10 or 20), lesser of CV and CL, CL with Ey min, wet service factors, Cvr = 1.0 stated; simply supported, positive bending. Validation: Boise GLM-5000 24F-V4 allowable shear and moment (4 sizes, exact) and its span note; hand calculation. Not built: glulam columns (Table 5B), negative bending / continuous spans, Cvr 0.72 at connections, Southern Pine shear increase. 237 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Glulam-Beam.pdf`.
- **0.34.0 (2026-11-23): Weyerhaeuser beams.** Screen "Microllam, Parallam or TimberStrand Beam" (`wey_beam`); `scl.beam_spec(product, ...)` makes `ws_wood.lvl_beam` product-neutral (`product=`). ICC-ES ESR-1387 Table 1 joist values typed into `data/esr1387_table1.csv` (47 grades), notes 5, 7, 8, 11, 15, 21. Validation: the report's own CV table (37 values), note 5 deflection, hand calculation; **no independent manufacturer table on file** (a Weyerhaeuser beam guide such as TJ-9000 would give one). Not built: Weyerhaeuser columns (Parallam note 13 eccentricity), plank orientation. 240 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Parallam-Beam.pdf`.
- **0.35.0 (2026-11-23): I-joists.** `ijoist.py`, `ws_ijoist.py`, screen "I-Joist": Boise BCI (ESR-1336; the 5 series of the West guide: 5000-1.7, 6000-1.8, 6500-1.8, 60-2.0, 90-2.0) and Weyerhaeuser TJI (ESR-1153; 110, 210, 230, 360, 560, 560D), data by `tools/parse_ijoists.py` into `data/ijoists.csv`. NDS Ch. 7: moment, shear, end reaction (bearing length, web stiffeners), deflection with shear term 8M/K; compression flange must be supported; simple spans. Validation: all 19 BCI rows equal Boise guide MTP-W7000 design properties; live load at L/480 equals the guide's load table (3 spans, within 1 plf); TJI by hand with the report's formula (no TJI guide on file). A sheet may set `sheet.code` to override the footer code line. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-I-Joist.pdf`.
- **0.36.0 (2026-11-23): Weyerhaeuser columns.** Screen `wey_post`; `scl.column_spec` makes `ws_wood.lvl_post` product-neutral. Parallam grades with Fc 2,700 or 2,900 take the minimum eccentricity e2 = t/6 + 5L^2/4608 (ESR-1387 note 13) automatically. Hand-calculation validation only. 245 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Parallam-Column.pdf`.
- **0.37.0 (2026-11-23): lateral load on composite lumber columns.** `lvl_post(..., lateral=)` and a lateral table on both column screens: NDS Eq. 15.4-1 / 15.4-2 (`scl.eccentric_column` takes fb1, fb2), shear. Versa-Lam columns now take joist Fb/Fv about the strong axis and plank about the weak axis; square sections take plank about both (veneers lie parallel to the wide face). Hand-calculation validation. 246 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-LVL-Column-With-Wind.pdf`.
- **Glulam columns HELD (2026-11-23).** Trial against Boise GLM-5000 "Combination 3" column tables (Fc 2,300, Fbx 2,000, Fby 2,100, Emin 1.0e6, e = d/6, **2024 NDS**): 2018 NDS Eq. 15.4-3 with Fb = 2,000 lands within about 3 percent for 3-1/8, 3-1/2 and 5-1/8 x 5-1/8 and 5-1/8 x 6 columns, but is about 10 percent ABOVE the table for 5-1/8 x 7-1/2 (e.g. 12 ft, 100 percent: 21,377 lb against 19,400). Cause not found (the table is not monotonic in stress between 5-1/8 x 6 and 5-1/8 x 7-1/2). Not released until explained; Zach told.
- **0.38.0 (2026-11-23 10:05, Zach asked for loading diagrams on beams and columns like the roof snow one).** The elevation on every wood, LVL/PSL/LSL, glulam and I-joist beam sheet and every wood and composite post sheet is now a LOADING diagram: each uniform load its own band of arrows over its extent with case and plf, point loads as arrows with case and lb (loads at one point combined in one label), axial loads listed over the post, lateral load arrows on the narrow face. Picture dict keys: `loads` (beams), `axial`, `lateral` (posts). Steel beam sheets already had a loading sketch; steel columns, footings and frames not changed.

- **0.42.0 (2026-11-23): continuous spans and overhangs.** New `contbeam.py` (layout, live-load arrangements, envelope, deflection by span). `ws_wood.beam`, `lvl_beam` (all four products) and `glulam_beam` take `spans=`, `overhang_left=`, `overhang_right=`, `lu_neg_ft=` (bottom edge unbraced length where in compression; required when not a simple span), `bearing_int_in=`. Live load cases L and Lr are placed on every combination of spans and overhangs (ASCE/SEI 7-22 Sect. 4.3.3); negative moment checked with its own CL (glulam with Fbx-); interior bearing row; deflection per span, overhang limit on twice its length; picture shows every support. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-LVL-Beam-Two-Spans.pdf`.

Hand-calculation test of a two-span joist. 260 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-I-Joist-Two-Spans.pdf`.

- **0.47.0 (2026-11-23): snow drift and sliding snow.** `snow.lower_roof`, `ws_more.snow_drift_sheet`, screen "Snow Drift on a Lower Roof" (kind LOAD): leeward and windward drifts at a roof step (ASCE/SEI 7-22 Sect. 7.7.1, text confirmed against the page image) and sliding snow (Sect. 7.9: 0.4 pf W over 15 ft). Reading taken for the windward drift where it would be higher than the clear height: width 8 x (0.75 hd) as the text says, height capped at hc (the larger load of the readings). Validation: Commentary C7 Example 3 within 5 to 6 percent (the example mixes a 40 psf and a 32 psf ground load and prints hd = 4.55 ft where Eq. 7.6-1 gives 4.69 ft); hand calculations. Not built: separated structures, intersecting drifts, projections and parapets (7.8); the sheet's loads are not yet fed into members automatically. 269 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Snow-Drift.pdf`.
- **0.48.0 (2026-11-23): tapered loads.** A uniform or area load with a second value (`w2` / `q2`; screen column "Load at the To end") varies in a straight line between its ends, so a drift surcharge can be put on a joist, rafter or beam. Handled in `contbeam` (cut at supports with the taper kept), the load lists and the loading diagram. Tests: triangular-load statics, a joist under a drift by hand. 271 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Roof-Joist-Under-Drift.pdf`.
- **0.49.0 (2026-11-23): parapet and roof projection drifts.** The snow drift screen has "Drift at: a roof step / a parapet or roof projection"; `snow.lower_roof(..., parapet=True)` gives the one windward-method drift of ASCE/SEI 7-22 Sect. 7.8. Hand-calculation test. 272 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Snow-Drift-Parapet.pdf`.
- **0.50.0 (2026-11-23): eccentric load on sawn posts.** `ws_wood.post(..., e1_in=, e2_in=)` and two fields on the Wood Post screen: NDS Eq. 15.4-3 (15.4-1 with lateral load) through `scl.eccentric_column`; the existing lateral-load tests still pass, which confirms Eq. 15.4-1 reduces to Eq. 3.9-3 at zero eccentricity. Bending toward the wide face uses the edgewise Fb with no flat use increase; not offered for Beams and Stringers sizes. Hand-calculation test. 273 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Post-Eccentric.pdf`.
- **0.51.0 (2026-11-23): glulam columns, released on Zach's ruling of 11:57.** `glulam.column_values` (Table 5B, eight western combinations), `glulam.boise_column_load`, `ws_wood.glulam_post`, screen "Glulam Column": CP with c = 0.9, eccentric load by Eq. 15.4-3, and for Combination 3 columns of a size in Boise's GLM-5000 column tables (537 values read by `tools/parse_glm5000.py`, 13 sizes) a third row limiting the axial load to the table value. Table entered with the longer effective length rounded up to a whole foot; CD 0.9 uses 0.9 x the 100 percent value, CD 1.6 the 125 percent value. Depth limited to 15 in.; dry service; no lateral load. Tests: Table 5B and table data spot checks, the calculation against the table where they agree (within 1.2 percent), a sheet where the table governs, hand CP. 276 tests pass. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Glulam-Column.pdf`.
- **0.52.0 (2026-11-23): sloped rafters.** `rise_on_12=` on `ws_wood.beam`, `lvl_beam`, `glulam_beam` and `ws_ijoist.joist` (screen field "Roof rise in 12"): analysis on the horizontal projection; uniform dead load is entered along the slope and divided by cos; shear x cos; deflection / cos^2 against the sloped length over the ratio. Works with multiple spans and overhangs. Not checked (listed on the sheet): axial force and thrust, wind normal to the roof, bearing on a sloped seat. The picture is still drawn level. Not built: a nail picker from Table L4, toe-nails (geometry of 12.1.6.3, Ctn 0.83), bolts on a screen (need Cg by Eq. 11.3-1 and the geometry factor of 12.5.1), lag and wood screws, withdrawal, a sketch of the connection. Opened in a headless browser. 333 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Nailed-Connection.pdf`.
- 0.78.1 / 0.78.2 (2026-11-23): dimensioned section sketch on the nailed connection sheet (`_nailed_picture`); `dowel.group_action` (NDS Eq. 11.3-1, read from the page image, pdf p. 80), **reproduces 10 values of the published Table 11.3.6A within 0.006**. For the bolted connection screen still to read: geometry factor and the spacing, end and edge distance tables (NDS 12.5.1, Tables 12.5.1A to 12.5.1D, about pdf pp. 98-100), then the screen. 344 tests.
- **0.79.0 (2026-11-23): bolted connections, load parallel to grain.** `ws_more.bolted_connection_sheet`, `dowel.geometry_parallel`, `dowel.edge_minimum`, screen "Bolted Connection (load parallel to grain)" (kind CONN, mark BC): single or double shear, rows of bolts 1/2 to 1 in., yield modes printed, Cg by Eq. 11.3-1, geometry factor from end distance and spacing in a row (NDS 12.5.1.2, Tables 12.5.1A, 12.5.1B), CM (0.4 wet at fabrication and dry in service, 1.0 for one row, note 2; 0.7 wet in service), CD from the project's ASD combinations; rows for edge distance (Table 12.5.1C), spacing between rows (12.5.1D) and the 5 in. limit between outer rows (12.5.1.3); the sheet stops when a distance is under the C-delta 0.5 minimum or the rows do not fit the member. Layout sketch to scale with every distance dimensioned. Tables read from page images (document pp. 89-91, pdf pp. 101-103). **Double shear bolts reproduce the published Table 12F (5 bolts x 3 directions, within its 10 lb rounding).** Not built: load perpendicular or at an angle to grain, net section and tear-out of the members (Appendix E), staggered rows, steel side plates, lag screws. Opened in a headless browser. 350 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Bolted-Connection.pdf`.
- **0.80.0 (2026-11-23): member checks at bolted connections.** `dowel.local_stresses` and six optional "Tear-out" boxes on the bolted connection screen (Ft and Fv of the main and side members with every factor except CD, their source, hole diameter): net section tension, row tear-out and group tear-out by NDS Appendix E (Eq. E.2-1, E.3-2, E.3-3, E.4-1; document pp. 164-166, page images), one check row per member with the governing mode named; tension connections only. **Reproduces the standard's worked examples E.6 (34,424 / 7,163 / 18,393 lb) and E.7 (3,470 / 1,350 lb).** In the sample splice group tear-out governs (side members 9,944 lb, main member 11,601 lb) where the bolts alone gave 16,474 lb. Not built: taking Ft and Fv from the species tables, compression net section, staggered rows. 352 tests.
- 0.80.1 (2026-11-23): `dowel.NAILS` (NDS Appendix Table L4: 30 common, box and sinker nails, length and diameter, read from the document text) and a nail picker on the nailed connection screen, with "other (type the size)" for nails outside the table. Opened in a headless browser. 352 tests.
- **0.81.0 (2026-11-23): toe-nails.** "Toe-nailed: yes" on the nailed connection screen: ls = the lesser of the side member thickness and L/3 (NDS 12.3.10.2), Ctn = 0.83 (12.5.4.2), nail at 30 degrees started L/3 from the member end (12.1.6.3, Figure 12A). **Reading told to Zach 14:02:** the standard gives no equation for the toe-nail's penetration into the main member; the sheet uses the geometry of Figure 12A, p = L cos 30 - L/3 (the projection of the nail along the side member, as in AWC's published examples), stated as an assumption on the sheet. No section sketch for toe-nails yet. Hand-calculation test. 353 tests.
- **0.82.0 (2026-11-23): hip roofs, main wind system, on Zach's ruling of 14:03** ("I agree with your hip roof design"; he also agreed the toe-nail reading). `wind.mwfrs_hip` and `mwfrs(..., roof="hip", theta_hip=)`; screen choice "hip" with the hip end roof angle. For each wind direction: the plane facing the wind takes the windward roof Cp at its own slope, the opposite plane the leeward Cp, the planes at the sides the zone coefficients by distance from the windward edge; walls run to the eave; the horizontal roof force acts on the facing plane's projected area (trapezoid for wind normal to the ridge, triangle for wind on a hip end); a facing slope under 10 degrees goes by zones only. Picture: triangle section across the ridge, trapezoid section along it. Hand-calculation test. Hip roof components and cladding (Figures 30.3-2E to 2G) not built. Opened in a headless browser. 354 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wind-Hip-Roof.pdf`.
- **0.83.0 (2026-11-23): seismic system table.** `seismic.SYSTEMS` / `seismic.system`: 15 rows of ASCE/SEI 7-22 Table 12.2-1 (wood and other light-frame bearing walls, reinforced masonry shear walls, wood and masonry building frames, steel SCBF and OCBF, steel special, intermediate and ordinary moment frames, steel ordinary cantilever columns, timber frames, steel not specifically detailed), read from 240 dpi page images turned upright (`refs/asce7/R173r.png` to `R175r.png`; the table is printed sideways on pdf pp. 173-175). Seismic screen: "Seismic force-resisting system" picker fills R and Cd with the table line as their source; the sheet adds a structural height row against the limit for the Seismic Design Category, flags a system that is not permitted except by a table footnote (notes j, k, l are named, not evaluated), and stops for a system not permitted outright. "other" keeps the typed R and Cd. **Left out on purpose:** steel special cantilever column systems (line G.1): the scan appears to print an overstrength factor of 2-1/2 where 1-1/4 was expected; not entered until read from a clean copy. Opened in a headless browser. 355 tests.
- **0.84.0 (2026-11-23): bolts loaded perpendicular to grain.** The bolted connection screen (now "Bolted Connection") has "Load direction to the grain": parallel in every member, perpendicular in the side member(s), or perpendicular in the main member, with the loaded edge, unloaded edge and end distance of the perpendicular member. Z with Fe perpendicular (Hankinson at 90 degrees) and Rd with K-theta 1.25; end distance factor of the perpendicular member (2 D to 4 D); rows for loaded edge 4 D, unloaded edge 1.5 D, the 5 in. limit across the grain, and row spacing by l/D (Table 12.5.1D); second sketch of the perpendicular member, dimensioned. Zs-perpendicular and Zm-perpendicular already match Tables 12A and 12F in the tests. **Reading told to Zach 14:20:** for the group action factor NDS 11.3.6.3 gives the perpendicular member's area as thickness x width of the bolt group, and for a single row "the minimum parallel to grain spacing", which a single row along the load does not have; StruKit uses the lesser of the spacing in the row and 4 D (the smaller area, lower Cg). Not built: shear at the connection in the perpendicular member (NDS 3.4.3.3), tear-out for these cases, load at other angles. Opened in a headless browser. 356 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Bolted-Connection-Perpendicular.pdf`.
- **0.85.0 (2026-11-23): shear at a bolted connection.** `dowel.shear_at_connection` and the boxes "Perpendicular member: Fv" and its source: NDS 3.4.3.3, Eq. 3.4-6 within 5 d of the member end and Eq. 3.4-7 beyond (document pp. 18-19, page images), de = depth less the unloaded edge distance; the shear is taken as the whole connection load (stated). In the first sample (2,000 lb beam reaction on two 3/4 in. bolts in 2 - 2x10) this check fails (1,443 lb) where the bolts pass (2,320 lb): **the sample sent at 14:12 without this check showed "OK"; the sample was re-issued with the check and a 1,300 lb load.** Hand-calculation test. 357 tests.
- **CMU walls blocked (2026-11-23 16:33, no build):** the copy of TMS 402/602-16 in the reference folder (`715304102-TMS-402-602-16-Masonry-Code.pdf`, 383 pages) cannot be read here. It is a sign-in protected PDF: every page shows a blank cover until the reader signs in with the licence it was issued under, and ordinary text extraction and page rendering return nothing. StruKit does not work around the protection. Earlier log entries that called it "page images, needing OCR" were wrong: there is nothing to OCR. CMU walls wait for a copy that opens normally (a purchased TMS 402-16, or the 2022 edition if Zach prefers). Told to Zach at the next check-in. A scratch copy of the file sits in the cloud workspace at `refs/tms` (not in the repo); it could not be removed from there by the chat and holds nothing usable.
- **0.119.0 (2026-11-23): Mixed Southern Pine.** 23 rows added to `nds2018_table4b.csv` (Select Structural, No. 1, No. 2, No. 3 and Stud in five width classes; Construction, Standard, Utility at 4 in. wide), parsed from the Supplement text and spot-checked; `wood.SP_SPECIES`; the species is on the Wood Beam, Wood Post and Built-Up Wood Column screens with the same Table 4B rules as Southern Pine. Test against values read from the Supplement plus a hand calculation. Opened in a headless browser. 387 tests. Still out: the Dense Structural 86/72/65 grades and Spruce Pine (the conversion factors of Table 4B note 3).
- **0.118.0 (2026-11-23): lumber decking.** New screen "Lumber Decking" (`decking`), `ws_wood.decking`, `wood.DECKING` from `strukit/data/nds2018_table4e.csv` (66 rows, 27 species; parsed from the Supplement text and spot-checked against it; Redwood and Southern Pine decking not included). A strip 12 in. wide, 2, 3 or 4 in. nominal, loads in psf, simple span or two equal spans. Bending with the Table 4E value (single member Fb or repetitive (Fb)(Cr)), CD, CM and the size factor by thickness (1.10, 1.04, 1.0); deflection limits as on the beam screens. Two equal spans: M = w L^2 / 8 over the middle support; deflection from dead load on both spans (w L^4 / 185 E I) plus each other load on one span alone (0.0092 w L^4 / E I). Shear is not checked: Table 4E lists no shear value (stated on the sheet). No sketch on this sheet yet. Hand-calculation test. Opened in a headless browser. 386 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Lumber-Decking.pdf`.
- **0.117.0 (2026-11-23): loads on the frame elevation.** Steel moment frame sheets: the elevation now carries every load by load case (`calcsheet._frame_loads`): member gravity loads as rows of arrows with one label per load at its left end (kip/ft), sideways member loads, and joint and level loads as single arrows on the outer side of the joint (kips). Arrows are not to a force scale (caption says so); the frame is drawn a little smaller to make room. Printed-sheet change only. Test prints every load type. 385 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Steel-Frame.pdf`.
- **0.116.0 (2026-11-23): loads on the footing-with-moment section.** The governing service case is drawn on the section: P down the column, M as a curved arrow toward the edge with the larger soil pressure, V at the top of the footing, each with its value; the caption names the case. Printed-sheet change only. 384 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Footing-With-Moment.pdf`. With 0.115.0 and 0.117.0 this completes Zach's loading-diagram rule across footings and frames.
- **0.115.0 (2026-11-23): loading on the footing sketches.** Isolated and continuous footing sheets: the section sketch now shows the service load as an arrow down the column or stem wall, with its value, and the soil pressure as arrows under the footing (`calcsheet._footing_load`); the caption says the arrows are not to a force scale. Printed-sheet change only; the section is drawn a little smaller to make room. 384 tests. Samples: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Isolated-Footing.pdf`, `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Continuous-Footing.pdf`. 
- **Wind validation search (2026-11-23 16:17, no build):** looked on the web for a published ASCE/SEI 7-22 main-system worked example to test against. The one found with full numbers (CED Engineering course S05-018, Sect. 2.3.1 and 3.2) is not usable: its Exposure C coefficient near the ground is 0.70 where Table 26.10-1 gives 0.85, it carries Kd inside the velocity pressure (the 7-16 form), and its second example does not reconcile with itself. Wind stays validated by hand calculation against the standard's own tables and figures. Still wanted: the ASCE "Wind Loads: Guide to the Wind Load Provisions of ASCE 7-22" or the ICC "Structural Load Determination: 2024 IBC and ASCE/SEI 7-22" (both sold, neither on file).
- **0.114.0 (2026-11-23): shear and moment diagrams on posts with lateral load.** Wood Post and LVL post sheets print the shear and moment diagram of the combination with the largest lateral moment, drawn along the height with the base at the left (heading says so); posts with axial load only are unchanged. One assumption sentence reworded on the post sheet ("with no flat use increase" -> "(Cfu = 1.0)"). Printed-sheet change only. Hand-calculation test (6x6, 10 ft, 0.6 x 120 plf: 900 lb-ft, 360 lb). 384 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Post-Lateral.pdf`. Glulam and built-up posts take no lateral load on their screens.
- **0.113.0 (2026-11-23): reduced live load carried into members.** The same five beam and joist screens gain "Take a reduced live load from [live load sheet] [member] - Add Live Load". `/api/live_loads` lists each floor member (case L) and roof member (case Lr) of a Live Load Reduction sheet with its reduced load; the screen adds one loads row (area load in psf, note "sheet LL1, member B3: reduced live load") and replaces it on a second press. Pressed twice in a headless browser. 383 tests. Posts and columns take point loads, so nothing is carried into them.
- **0.112.0 (2026-11-23): snow load carried into members.** Wood Beam, LVL, glulam, Weyerhaeuser beam and I-joist screens: new bar "Take the uniform snow load from [roof snow sheet] - Add Snow Load". `/api/snow_loads` returns the sheet's uniform design snow load (the larger of the balanced case and the minimum) and which governs; the screen adds one loads row (case S, area load in psf, note "sheet SN1: balanced snow load ps") and replaces it on a second press. The load is on the horizontal projection, as the member screens take area loads. Unbalanced, drift and sliding loads are not carried over (the user is told). Pressed twice in a headless browser. 382 tests.
- **0.111.0 (2026-11-23): sheet wording review.** Every long sentence the sheets print (assumptions, notes, not-checked lists, step notes) in the steel beam, steel column, frame, weld and bolt, base plate, anchorage and footing modules was listed and read against the 2026-11-23 06:45 rule (a sheet states what was used and its reference, nothing else), and the wood, snow, wind and seismic modules were searched for rationale wording. Four sentences reworded: wind main system "The area reduction of the -1.3 roof value is not taken" -> "The roof value Cp = -1.3 is used as tabulated"; snow gap "The windward drift is not truncated" -> "The windward drift is taken at its full width (Sect. 7.7.1)"; base plate source "(as printed in AISC Design Examples v16.0, Example J.6)" removed; footing with moment note "Many soils reports and codes limit this" removed. Everything else read as a stated design assumption or a code reference. No calculation changed. 381 tests. This closes the open item "sheets built before 06:45 have not all been re-read".
- **0.110.0 (2026-11-23): drift check filled from shear wall deflections.** Seismic Base Shear screen: new bar "Drift: take a story deflection from [shear wall] [story below level] - Fill Displacement". `/api/wall_deflection` returns the largest segment deflection of a Wood Shear Wall or perforated wall sheet at the project's load level; the screen divides it by 0.7 for an ASD project (the wall sheet's shear is 0.7 rho QE), adds it to the displacement of the level below (levels fill from the base up; a blank level below is refused) and writes the source into "where the elastic displacements come from" (for example "story below Roof: shear wall SW2, 0.184 in. at ASD level divided by 0.7"). **The redundancy factor stays in the deflection** (Sect. 12.3.4.1 permits rho = 1.0 for drift; not taken - the conservative reading, stated to Zach 15:46, "Sounds good" 16:05). The anchorage slip typed on the wall sheet is scaled with the rest. One wall line per story: the user picks the one with the largest deflection. Run end to end in a headless browser (two walls, two levels: 0.320 and 0.583 in.; drifts 1.28 and 1.05 in. with Cd = 4). 381 tests.
- **0.109.0 (2026-11-23): sliding snow across a gap.** The Snow Drift screen now takes sliding snow together with a gap: ASCE/SEI 7-22 Sect. 7.9 for separated structures (page text, pdf pp. 128-129): considered when h/s is over 1 and s is under 15 ft; intensity 0.4 pf W / 15 over the extent 15 - s on the lower roof (load per foot of eave 0.4 pf W (15 - s)/15); otherwise none, with the reason printed. Hand-calculation test. Checked in a headless browser. 380 tests.
- **0.108.0 (2026-11-23): snow drift on an adjacent lower structure.** New optional box "Gap between the two structures" on the Snow Drift screen; `snow.lower_roof(..., separation=)`. ASCE/SEI 7-22 Sect. 7.7.2 (page text, pdf p. 126): where the gap s is under 20 ft and under 6 h, the leeward drift on the lower structure has the height min(hd, (6h - s)/6) and the extent min(6 x that height, 6h - s); the windward drift follows Sect. 7.7.1 and its permitted truncation is not taken; otherwise no drift is required and the sheet says why. The hc/hb 0.2 exemption is not applied to separated structures. Sketch shows the higher wall standing back by the gap (not to scale). Not built with a gap: sliding snow across it (Sect. 7.9), parapets. Hand-calculation test. Opened in a headless browser. 379 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Snow-Drift-Adjacent-Structure.pdf`.
- **0.107.0 (2026-11-23): washer bearing, lag screws in withdrawal.** Five optional boxes on the Lag Screws in Withdrawal screen (washer outside diameter, hole, side member Fc_perp and its source, whether the washer is 3 in. or more from the member end). Bearing stress under a round washer on its net area against Fc_perp', with the bearing area factor Cb = (lb + 0.375) / lb, lb = the washer diameter, taken only when the washer is 3 in. or more from the end (NDS 3.10.2, 3.10.4); otherwise Cb = 1.0. Fc_perp is used as entered (no CD applies to it). Hand-calculation test. Opened in a headless browser. 378 tests.
- **0.106.0 (2026-11-23): shear walls with different sheathing on the two sides.** Wood Shear Wall screen: "Panels on" gains "both sides (different panels or nailing)" with three boxes for the other side; `shearwall.line(..., other=dict(panel, spacing, panel_type))`. SDPWS-2021 4.3.5.4.2: combined vn = the greater of two times the smaller and the larger; combined Ga = two times that of the side with the smaller vn, or that of the side with the larger, to match. Blocked walls; segments of one line must be the same length (shear distribution, 4.3.5.5.1); the gypsum wallboard exception is not built (no gypsum in StruKit). Identical sides entered this way agree with the 4.3.5.4.1 result. Hand-calculation test. Opened in a headless browser. 377 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Shear-Wall-Two-Sheathings.pdf`. Not on the perforated wall screen.
- **0.105.0 (2026-11-23): collector screen fills from a diaphragm.** `fillCollectorBar` in app.js: pick a Wood Diaphragm sheet and press "Fill Force and Length"; the reaction (w L / 2), the diaphragm width W, the load and load level, and the source text are filled in. `/api/diaphragm_reaction` now also returns W. Pressed in a headless browser (4,000 lb, 24 ft, collector 2,667 lb). 376 tests.
- **0.104.0 (2026-11-23): collector force.** New screen "Collector Force (drag strut)" (`collector`), `diaphragm.collector`, `ws_more.collector_sheet`, `calcsheet._collector_picture`. For one line of resistance: the diaphragm shear delivered uniformly along the line (vd = R / W), the walls on the line taking it in proportion to length (vw), and the collector force as the running sum along the line, tabulated at every wall end and drawn as a force diagram over the line with the walls drawn heavy and dimensioned. The force is at the load level of the force entered; overstrength (Sect. 12.10.2.1) is applied in the member design through load case Em. Hand-calculation test. Opened in a headless browser. 375 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Collector-Force.pdf`. Not built: filling the force from a diaphragm sheet, the collector member check itself (use the wood or steel member screens with the force typed in).
- **0.103.0 (2026-11-23): example project combinations corrected.** The built-in example project carried four ASD and four LRFD combinations typed in the older form (D + S, D + 0.75L + 0.75S + 0.45W, 1.2D + 1.6S + 0.5W ...) with the source "ASCE/SEI 7 Section 2.4 / 2.3 (typed in)". They are now the ASCE/SEI 7-22 forms (D + 0.7S, D + 0.75L + 0.45W + 0.525S, 1.2D + 1.0S + 0.5W, 1.2D + 1.6L + 0.3S ...) with the edition in the source. Only the example project was affected; the "Fill both lists" button has always given the 7-22 list. Example reloaded in a headless browser: all 11 members OK. 374 tests. Told to Zach at the 15:30 check-in.
- **0.102.0 (2026-11-23): seismic combinations with overstrength.** Project page: new optional box "Overstrength factor"; the seismic combinations button then also adds Sect. 2.3.6 combinations 6 and 7 and Sect. 2.4.5 combinations 8, 9 and 10 with Emh = Omega0 QE (Sect. 12.4.3) to both lists, written on a separate load case, **Em** (for example 1.14D + 1.75Em for SDS 1.0, Omega0 2.5). A member that must be designed for overstrength carries its seismic load (QE, the same number as under E) under load case Em in place of E; every other member is unaffected, so overstrength is per member with no change to any member screen. The redundancy factor does not enter these combinations. The 1.2 allowable stress increase permitted by Sect. 2.4.5 is not taken. `loadcombos.seismic(..., omega0=)`; `wood.CD["Em"]` = 1.6. Combination text confirmed from the page text (pdf pp. 69-70). Test by hand; Project page opened in a headless browser (button pressed twice, lists stable). 374 tests. One Omega0 per project; a project with two systems needs the second set typed in.
- **0.101.0 (2026-11-23): section sketch on the lag screw lateral sheet.** The lag screw section (side member or plate, head, shank, shaded threads, tip; side thickness and penetration p dimensioned; load arrows on each member) now prints after the layout sketch on every lag screw lateral sheet. Printed-sheet change only. 373 tests. Samples re-issued: Lag-Screw-Connection, Lag-Screw-Combined-Loading.
- **0.100.0 (2026-11-23): steel side plates, main member loaded perpendicular to grain.** Bolted and lag screw screens: with a steel side plate the load direction may be "perpendicular in the main member" (hanger and ledger plates). No wood member is loaded parallel to grain, so the end distance, edge distance, load sense and wood type boxes are not used (now optional on both screens; still required whenever a wood member is loaded parallel). Geometry factor from the end distance of the wood member (Table 12.5.1A); spacing in a row not less than 3 D, with the spacing for 1.0 that required for the steel plate (Table 12.5.1B); loaded and unloaded edge distances, 5 in. limit across the grain, shear at the connection (3.4.3.3). Reproduces Table 12B perpendicular value. Opened in a headless browser. 373 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Bolted-Steel-Plate-Perpendicular.pdf`. (Build numbers run 0.99.0, 0.100.0: the updater copies the latest build and does not compare numbers.)
- **0.99.0 (2026-11-23): Southern Pine posts and built-up columns.** `wood.post_values` takes Southern Pine from Table 4B (CF = 1.0; 0.9 on Fc for widths over 12 in.; wet service factor on Fc judged on Fc alone, as Table 4B words it); species and grades added to the Wood Post and Built-Up Wood Column screens; sheet references read "Table 4B". Size adjustment is in the tabulated values; CF = 1.1 on Fb for 4 in. thick, 8 in. and wider; wider than 12 in.: the 12 in. values with CF = 0.9 (Table 4B adjustment factors). Beams, joists, rafters and planks only: Southern Pine posts and studs, Mixed Southern Pine and the Dense Structural grades are not built. - **0.97.0 (2026-11-23): flat use (planks).** New box "Loaded on the wide face (flat use, plank)" on the Wood Beam screen; `ws_wood.beam(..., flat=True)`, `wood.flat_use` (Cfu, 2018 NDS Supplement Table 4A adjustment factors, read from the Supplement text). One piece of dimension lumber 2 to 4 in. thick; the section is turned (b = the wide face), Fb is multiplied by Cfu as well as CF, CL = 1.0 (depth does not exceed breadth). Hand-calculation test. Opened in a headless browser. 369 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Plank-Flat-Use.pdf`. Decking (Table 4E) not built.
- **0.96.0 (2026-11-23): wood screws in withdrawal.** New screen "Wood Screws in Withdrawal" (`screw_withdrawal`), `ws_more.screw_withdrawal_sheet`, `dowel.wood_screw_withdrawal` (Eq. 12.2-2). Thread length 2/3 of the screw length (Table L3 notes); thread penetration = lesser of that and the length in the main member, tip not deducted (12.2.2.2 does not exclude it, unlike 12.2.1.2 for lag screws); head pull-through of a wood side member with the Table L3 head diameter (12.2.5); optional screw tension row; steel side member option. End grain not offered (Ceg = 0). Checked against Table 12.2B (within 1 lb/in.) and by hand. Opened in a headless browser. 368 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Screws-in-Withdrawal.pdf`.
- **Sample correction (2026-11-23 14:57):** the first copies of three samples (Lag-Screw-Connection, Lag-Screw-Combined-Loading, Wood-Screw-Connection) were printed with a hand-typed combination "D + S" labelled "ASCE/SEI 7-22 Sect. 2.4.1, combination 3". That combination in ASCE/SEI 7-22 is D + 0.7S. The program's own combination list was right (the screens showed D + 0.7S); only the sample inputs were wrong. All three re-issued with D + 0.7S; test fixtures relabelled "test combination". Told to Zach at the 14:58 check-in.
- **0.95.0 (2026-11-23): wood screw connections, lateral.** New screen "Wood Screw Connection (lateral)" (`screw_connection`), #6 to #14 (Table L3; larger numbers are 1/4 in. and over and are not offered); `nailed_connection_sheet(..., screw="#10")`, `dowel.wood_screw`, `wood_screw_fyb`, `WOOD_SCREWS`. Yield equations use the root diameter Dr; Fyb goes by the screw diameter D - this reproduces Table 12L exactly (nine rows checked). Penetration 6 D minimum (12.1.5.6), lm = p less half the tip. Opened in a headless browser. 367 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Wood-Screw-Connection.pdf`. Wood screws in withdrawal (Eq. 12.2-2) not built.

The force entered is the resultant. Hand-calculation test (30, 90 and 0 degrees). Opened in a headless browser. 366 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Lag-Screw-Combined-Loading.pdf`.

New box "Side member material" (wood or steel; for the sketch only). 365 tests. Sample re-issued: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Lag-Screws-in-Withdrawal.pdf`.
- **0.92.0 (2026-11-23): steel side plates.** New box "Side member material" (wood, ASTM A36 steel plate, ASTM A653 Grade 33 steel sheet) on the bolted and lag screw screens; `bolted_connection_sheet(..., steel=...)`, `dowel.STEEL_FE` (87,000 and 61,850 psi), wood-to-metal load/slip modulus in the group action factor. Load parallel to the grain of the main member only (perpendicular with steel plates not built). Tear-out and net section checked for the main member only; the plate itself is listed as not checked (AISC 360). Reproduces NDS Table 12B (four rows) and Table 12K (six rows), both load directions, within rounding. Opened in a headless browser. 365 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Bolted-Connection-Steel-Side-Plate.pdf`.
- **0.91.0 (2026-11-23): lag screws in withdrawal.** New screen "Lag Screws in Withdrawal" (`lag_withdrawal`), `ws_more.lag_withdrawal_sheet`: W = 1800 G^1.5 D^0.75 (Eq. 12.2-1) times the thread penetration (lesser of the Table L2 minimum thread length and the length in the main member, less the tapered tip), CD, CM (Table 11.3.3), Ceg = 0.75 in end grain; optional rows for the screw's own allowable tension (entered with its source) and for edge distance, end distance and spacing against Table 12.5.1E. Checked against Tables 12.2A and L2. No sketch on this sheet yet. Opened in a headless browser. 364 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Lag-Screws-in-Withdrawal.pdf`.
- **0.90.0 (2026-11-23): lag screw connections, lateral.** New screen "Lag Screw Connection (lateral)" (`lag_connection`); `bolted_connection_sheet(..., lag=dict(L, washer))` so lag screws get the same group action, geometry factors, perpendicular-to-grain layout, edge checks and shear at the connection as bolts. `dowel.lag`, `lag_thread`, `lag_fyb`, `LAG_DR`, `LAG_E` (Table L2: 1/4 to 1 in., lengths 1 to 12 in.; T = lesser of 6 in. and L/2 + 1/2 in.). Yield equations and Rd use the root diameter Dr (12.3.7.1(c)); dowel bearing strengths use the nominal diameter; Rd = KD K-theta where Dr is under 1/4 in. and D is not (Table 12.3.1B note) - this reproduces Table 12J (nine rows checked, all four load directions, within rounding). Penetration p = L - washer - side member - tip E, 4 D minimum, lm = p. Single shear, wood side member only; net section and tear-out not offered for lag screws (listed as not checked). Opened in a headless browser. **Bug fixed:** a bolted connection with one bolt in a row and the spacing box left blank failed with an internal error in every build since the bolted screen was added; regression test added. 363 tests. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Lag-Screw-Connection.pdf`.

Wet service by Table 11.3.3 (four conditions; separate factors for withdrawal and pull-through); toe-nails: Ctn = 0.67, CM not applied (12.5.4.1), penetration L cos 30 - L/3 (the shorter of the two ways to measure it; consistent with the 14:03 ruling), pull-through not checked at toe-nails (listed). C8.2-1). Hydraulic head typed with its source or from a channel scupper (Design Guide 40 Eq. 1-7, cd 0.6). Validated against Design Guide 40 Example 1 (Q 149 gal/min, Cp 0.519, Cs 0.161, Bp 4.13, R 122 psf) and Example 2 (Q 304 gal/min, scupper dh 2.68 in.). Opened in a headless browser.

Schematic section sketch with H, Lh and x. **Reading applied under the standing conservative ruling:** K1/(H/Lh) is the larger of the exposure's value and the Exposure C value (the figure's table of multipliers is built on Exposure C and its note d says it is used for any exposure; the parameter table gives 1.30 for B and 1.55 for D on a ridge). Zach told at the 16:58 check-in. Validated against Wind Design Manual Example 2a (escarpment, Exposure D: Kzt 1.93) and 2b/2c (hill: Kzt 1.72) and against the figure's table values. The wind screens still take Kzt typed with its source.

- **0.163.0 (2026-11-23 19:03): two rulings of 19:00 built.** (1) **Stair tread plate, reduced width** ("Add a reduced width check. That's the right thing to do"): the tread plate between the legs (flat width b - 2t) is classed by AISC 360-22 Table B4.1b Case 17 (1.12 and 1.40 (E/Fy)^0.5); compact Mn = Mp; noncompact Eq. F7-2; slender Mn = Fy Se with the effective width of Eq. F7-4 (the HSS form, 0.38; the box form uses 0.34 and gives a little more width) and Se the lesser of the top and leg-tip moduli of the reduced section. Off the not-checked list. **Effect:** a slender tread loses the 1.6 Fy S plastic allowance. Design Guide 34 Example 10.2.2's tread (3/16 x 12, Fy 26.7 ksi, 1 kip) now FAILS: 0.560 kip-ft available against 0.761 (the Guide shows 0.909 and passes). An ordinary 3/16 x 12 A36 tread on a 3 ft stair under 300 lb still passes (236 against 748 lb-ft). No AISC provision names this shape; Sect. F7.2 is applied to the plate as the nearest stiffened-flange provision. (2) **Cladding on open hip roofs** ("Sure go number 2"): Figure 30.5-2 on each roof plane at its own slope, two tables on the hip sheet (main planes, hip-end planes), eaves, hips and ridge taken as edges; printed as a Thatcher Engineering standard. Tests updated and added. 458 tests. - **0.164.0 (2026-11-23 19:40): job 2026-011 run whole (Zach 19:33: "What if you ran it on 2026-011? And 2026-001?").** All 15 members of the sealed StruCalc package (4/21/2026) entered in one StruKit project (`tests/data/job_2026_011.strukit`, permanent test `tests/test_job_2026_011.py`); every member passes; 33 of 48 checks equal StruCalc within 0.6 percent; the other 15 differ for recorded reasons (shear deflection of LVL and I-joists, live load arrangement on the three-span Floor Beam A, footing shear and stem wall weight by Zach's rulings). Package: 29 sheets. **What the run turned up, all fixed in this build:** (1) the BCI 4500S-1.8 joist was not in the data: added from ESR-1336 (11-7/8 in. only; its weight is the one StruCalc printed); (2) bearing-length interpolation for BCI end and intermediate reactions (ESR-1336 Sect. 4.2 permits it; StruKit had used the 1-1/2 in. value: 950 lb where StruCalc and the report give 1,306 lb at 3 in.); (3) the bearing area factor Cb at interior supports under 6 in. long (NDS 3.10.4, Eq. 3.10-2) on wood, LVL and glulam beams: StruKit had Cb = 1.0 and would have flagged the sealed Floor Beam A at 756.6 against 750 psi; (4) a load typed to 27.25 ft on spans typed 3 x 9.0833 ft stopped the run ("load is not on the beam"): loads and supports within 0.05 in. past the end now snap to the end; (5) the cover sheet's contents columns ran into each other with long marks and code lines. Also confirmed: StruCalc's built-up columns use Kf = 0.6 too (identical capacities), and StruCalc's continuous beam loads every span at once (StruKit with the same loading gives its 719.1 psi exactly).
- **0.165.0 (2026-11-23 19:47): StruCalc project reader, and job 2026-001's mezzanine.** New `strukit/strucalc.py`: reads a StruCalc Pro project file (.vtx, XML) into a StruKit project: steel floor beams of W, M, S, HP, channel and HSS shapes (spans, free ends as overhangs, uniform and point loads, top flange bracing, deflection limits, self-weight) and HSS or pipe columns (axial load, end moments, unbraced lengths as K factors), with StruCalc's own demand and strength for every check kept beside each member and a note for everything not carried across. The window's Open now takes .vtx files (opens as a new unsaved project; the StruCalc file is never written to). **Job 2026-001 mezzanine** (28 members in `2026-001-StruCalc.vtx`): 27 came across and ran (`tests/data/job_2026_001_mezzanine.strukit`, `tests/test_job_2026_001.py`); 78 of 80 comparable checks equal StruCalc within 0.8 percent (shear, flexure, compression, interaction, deflection; W6 to W18, HSS); package 56 sheets. **Turned up and fixed:** the HSS column used Cm = 1.0 always and flagged Column-A.1 at 1.175 where StruCalc has 0.978; new optional box "End moments: smaller over larger, M1 / M2" gives Cm = 0.6 - 0.4 (M1 / M2) (AISC 360-22 Eq. A-8-4), blank keeps Cm = 1.0; with the moment at the top only StruKit gives 0.9784, StruCalc's number. **Gaps the job shows, not built:** (1) steel beams with a weak-axis or torsional moment (FloorBeamD.3, FloorBeamY.5: StruCalc checks H1-1b with Moment X); (2) single angles as beams (the L8x8x3/4, AISC F10); (3) the crane runway, its bracing and splices, and the vestibule are not in the StruCalc file; their sealed sheets are drawings (images), so there are no calcs on file to run against, and StruKit has no crane load or underhung runway (bottom flange bending) check; (4) wood, footing and other StruCalc member types are not read from .vtx yet (2026-011 was typed in by hand). FloorBeamY.3 fails in both programs (a trial size in the working file). 462 tests (the two job tests add about 7 s).

## 2026-11-23 23:45 — 0.176.0 takedown links stored and recalculated (492 tests)
- **Stored links.** "Add Reaction" and a footing's "Fill Loads" now store a link with the loads (on each generated load row as `link`: src mark, support name, count, x, and for a joist row `mode: "line"`, `spacing_in`; on a footing as `values.link`). Links are kept by member mark and are saved in the project file.
- **`calc.takedown(project, members)` and `/api/takedown`:** sorts the linked members from the top of the load path down, rewrites every linked member's rows (or a footing's service and factored loads) from the member above as it stands at that moment, so a change at the top reaches the footing in one pass. Stops and changes nothing if the links form a circle; reports a link whose member above has been removed or renamed and leaves those loads as they were. Running it twice changes nothing.
- **Project page: "Load path"** card with the button "Recalculate the load path" and a list of every link (member, takes the reaction of, at, count, load).
- **A row of joists or rafters as a load per foot:** when the member above has a spacing, the bar offers "a row of them, as a load per foot": the reaction by case x 12 / spacing as uniform loads over the beam (or from a typed position). Check on the 2026-011 file: the floor joist row gives 297.1 plf live on Floor Beam B, the 297 plf typed from the sealed package.

## 2026-11-24 00:15 — 0.177.0 building model, first step (495 tests)
- **Project page: "Building Model" card** (optional): levels (lowest first, with elevations), grid lines in X and Y, the button "Link members by position", and a plan view of any level (grids, beams and joists as lines with their marks, posts and columns as squares, footings dashed). Stored in the project file as `project.model` = dict(levels, grids_x, grids_y).
- **Placement boxes on every beam, joist, post, column and footing screen** (optional; not part of the calculation): level, and X / Y of the position (both ends for a beam), typed in feet or as a grid name with an optional offset ("B", "B+2.5"; a grid name wins over a number). Stored on the member as `at_level`, `at_base`, `at_x1`, `at_y1`, `at_x2`, `at_y2`.
- **`calc.model_links(project, members)` / `/api/model_links`:** under each support of a placed beam or joist, and under the base of each placed post or column, finds what carries it within 3 in. in plan: a post or column whose top is at that level, else a beam passing under the point (the reaction lands at the measured distance along it; a member with a spacing lands as a load per foot), else a footing. Makes the takedown links, replaces the positional links of the last run, keeps links picked by hand, then recalculates the load path top down. Reports: a support with nothing under it, more than one member under it, a member whose placed length does not match its spans, an unreadable position. `calc.model_plan` feeds the plan view.

- **`calc.lateral_wind(project, members)` / `/api/lateral_wind`, button "Send the wind to the wall lines":** flexible diaphragms. At each level the wind load per foot of building width comes from the project's main-system wind sheet between mid-story heights (level elevations; the top level runs to the top of the walls; the larger of the sheet's two wind directions; x 0.6 for an ASD project). For wind in X it goes to the wall lines running in X by tributary width between lines (to the edge of what is placed on that level at the outer lines); the same for Y. Walls on one line share by plan length. Each wall also takes the shear of the wall on the same line above and, for the wood shear wall and force-transfer wall, its largest chord force as the stacked hold-down force. The shear, its source text and the stacked force are written on each wall's own screen; a table on the Project page lists level, wind direction, wall, tributary width, shear, shear from above and chord force.
- Reports: a level without an elevation, no wind sheet, no wall in one direction, one wall line only (it takes everything), a wall above with no line under it (not carried down), walls higher than the wind sheet's.
- Test: 30 x 40 ft two-story box with an interior line at the lower story; shears equal the hand tributary values, the lower lines carry what comes from above, and the Y-direction lines at the floor add up to the whole load above mid-height of the first story. Run in a headless browser.
- **Not yet:** seismic story forces to the wall lines (the seismic sheet's wall-line table is still typed shares); rigid diaphragms and torsion; diaphragm sheets filled from the model; discontinuous walls (transfer through the diaphragm); roof slope and gable ends beyond what the wind sheet gives; wind on the lowest half-story (goes to the foundation).
Next: seismic to the wall lines by tributary area; diaphragm sheets from the model; then the 3D frame solver (extend frame2d to space frames, validated against AISC benchmark problems until RISA is available).

## 2026-11-24 02:00 — 0.181.0 3D frame from the building model (505 tests)
- **`calc.model_frame(project, members)` / `/api/model_frame`, button "Check the load path with a 3D frame analysis":** the placed steel beams and steel or HSS columns become one space frame (frame3d.py). Beams are cut at every point where a column or another beam meets them, at point loads and at the ends of partial uniform loads; beam ends are pinned (shear connections) and beams are continuous over what carries them; columns are continuous, pinned at the base (twist held); every joint at a level is held against sidesway (gravity analysis); a beam support with nothing under it bears on a support and is listed. Loads are each member's own typed loads and self-weight; a load row linked to a member that is itself in the frame is left out (that member brings it). Each load case is run alone.
- **Result:** the axial load at the base of every column by load case from the 3D frame, beside the load the takedown put on that column, with the difference (flagged over 5 percent). For simply connected framing the two must agree: on the one-bay test (four HSS columns, two girders, three infill beams) they agree within 0.2 percent (4,900 lb dead, 7,500 lb live per column), and the footings get 12,400 lb service.
- Wood members are named as not in the frame yet; the loads they put on steel members stay as the takedown loads.

## 2026-11-24 06:15 — 0.185.0 diagonal braces in the 3D frame (521 tests)

Value `at_brace`. Shown on the plan and in the 3D view.
- **In the sway run** a brace is pinned at both ends and carries axial force only (own weight left out). Each end must be on a placed column or beam (a beam is cut there), or come down to the lowest level, where it is held. Braces are not in the gravity check run (levels held).
- **Tension-only braces:** a brace works only while it is being stretched. The working set is found by repeating the first-order solution (a slack brace keeps a millionth of its area so its strain sign can be read), then confirmed on the second-order solution. Each run with tension-only braces also carries 0.0001 of the gravity load across its direction so one brace of each cross line engages (without it, both diagonals of a cross line go slack from column shortening and the second-order solution has nothing holding that line).
- **Brace results:** largest tension and compression with their runs; tension against yielding of the gross area (rods: also 0.75 Fu Ab), compression against Sect. E3 with the full length (W, HSS, channel) or the Single-Angle screen's own Sect. E5 calculation at the modelled length. A brace that goes into compression with no strength that can be worked out (for example an angle over Lc/r 200 or with slender legs) is reported as a problem. Net section rupture and connections stay on the brace's own screen.
- Tests: 20 x 20 ft bay braced on four faces with 3/4 in. rods: brace tension equals (notional load + P-Delta shear) / 2 / cos by statics within 1 percent, compression zero, column loads add up to the gravity load; the same bay with one L8x8x3/4 per face: mean of tension and compression equals the hand value; slender angle reported; brace end off the frame reported. A wide-flange beam continuous over a square HSS column, four bolts through the bottom flange and the cap plate, loads P (column compression) and M (moment across the joint) by load case. Checks: bolt tension from the couple (compression at the column face, 2T = (M - P B/2) / (B/2 + s/2)); beam flange and cap plate thickness for no prying (AISC Manual Eq. 9-34 with the tributary lengths of Figure 9-4 as the example uses them); bolt tension strength; beam web local yielding and crippling (J10-2, J10-4) for C = 2T + P on lb = 5 tp + t; HSS wall local yielding on 5 tp + 2 k1 + 5 tf and crippling for one wall; weld of the cap plate to the HSS by load per inch; Table J2.4 minimum weld. A flange or plate thinner than tnp is flagged (prying itself is not evaluated).
- **Validation: AISC Design Guide 24 (2nd Ed.) Example 6.1, LRFD and ASD, every published value** (T 7.60 / 5.05 kips, tnp 0.338 and 0.366 in., C 60.2 / 40.1 kips, 116 / 77.3, 113 / 75.0, 78.6 / 52.4, 79.5 / 53.0 kips, weld 2.17 / 1.44 kip/in.), within 1 percent (the example rounds 2T to 15.2).

## 2026-11-24 11:00 — 0.197.0 and 0.198.0 (537 tests)
- **0.197.0 cap plate screen filled from the frame.** The cap plate screen has a second table, "combined loads" (run name, column compression, moment), used in place of the load-case table when filled; bar "Take the joint loads from the 3D frame [column] [strong / weak-axis moment] - Fill Loads" writes the runs with the largest knee moment, largest and least column load from the top of that column in the sway run (`calc.frame_knee`, `/api/frame_knee`; each column of the run now carries `top`). Uplift on the column adds to the bolt tension and is not counted as compression at the wall.
- **0.198.0 base plate with biaxial bending, four rods.** New screen "Column Base Plate, Biaxial Bending (four rods)" (`base_plate_biaxial`, mark BPX), `ws_more.base_plate_biaxial_sheet`, `ws_more.biaxial_base_strength`, plan sketch to scale with plate size and rod edge distances. AISC Design Guide 1 (3rd Ed.) Sect. 4.3.8 (read from the text): at each case's axial compression the moment strength in each direction is found by raising the moment until the two rods on the tension side, or the plate at those rods (Eq. 4-62), reach their strength (Eq. 4-55, 4-58), then (Mrx / Mcx)^2 + (Mry / Mcy)^2 <= 1 (Eq. 4-69). A row checks that the plate is thick enough that the bearing side cannot yield (Eq. 4-51a); W shapes and square or rectangular HSS; ASD by the same equations with Omega. Axial tension is refused (the Guide's condition); shear must go by friction or a lug (stated).
- **Validation: Design Guide 1 Example 4.7-14.** With the Guide's own rod value (2 x 12.7 kips): qmax 61.9 kip/in., Y 1.86 in., Mc 837 kip-in. each way, plate limits 437 / 482 kip-in. and 303 / 213 kips, interaction 0.988. On the screen StruKit's rod strength is 0.75 x 0.75 Fu Ab (AISC 360-22 Table J3.2) = 12.9 kips against the Guide's Table 4-1 value of 12.7 kips, so the screen gives 0.97. **For a 5/8 in. rod the Specification value is the higher of the two (2 percent); for the 7/8 in. rod of the earlier examples it was the lower. Zach may want the lower of the two always; not changed.**
- Bar "Take the loads from the 3D frame [column] - Fill Loads" on this screen: runs with the largest strong-axis moment, largest weak-axis moment, largest and least compression, each with its concurrent P, Mx and My (`calc.frame_base_biaxial`). A run with uplift is outside this screen (the Base Plate with Moment and uplift screens take those).
- Samples: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Base-Plate-Biaxial.pdf`.

K1-2 width), HSS wall local crippling (Design Guide 24 Eq. 2-19); and the available moment of the connection.

Example 6.3, LRFD and ASD, every published value** (flat 7-3/4 in., Be 2.15 in., 73.1 / 48.7, 195 / 130 kips, U 0.875 / 0.973, Qf 0.808 / 0.753, 69.4 / 43.1, ceff 3.31 in., 112 / 74.7, 307 / 191 kips, 90.8 / 56.4 kip-ft), within 0.6 percent. ## 2026-11-25 14:20 local — 0.211.0 beam to HSS moment connection: filled from the frame, bolted flange plates (559 tests)
- Zach 13:33: "keep going" on the Next list, item 1.
- **Filled from the 3D frame.** Bar "Take the joint loads from the 3D frame [beam] [left / right end] - Fill Loads" on the Beam to HSS Column Moment Connection screen (`calc.frame_beam_hss`, `/api/frame_beam_hss`). It finds the frame column standing at that end of the beam (same level, within 3 in.), and from the same run takes the beam end moment, the column axial load and the column moment in the beam's plane at its top. It picks the runs with the largest beam moment, the largest column load and the largest column moment, and sets the face: a beam running along the column's depth frames into the B face (column strong axis), otherwise the H face. The beam and column sections fill when blank. Run in a headless browser on the eave portal carport (now stored as `tests/data/carport_eave_portals.strukit`): E3A left end, column C3A, face B, 10.1 kip-ft.
- **Flange plates bolted to the beam flange.** New optional boxes: flange plate Fu, beam Fu, bolt diameter, type and threads, bolts in each of two rows, spacing, gage, plate end to the last bolt, beam end to the first bolt, column face to the first bolt, fillet weld of the plate to the HSS face and electrode. With them the sheet adds nine rows for the largest moment: the bolts (each the least of shear, bearing and tearout in the plate and the beam flange; flange force M / d), plate tensile yielding and rupture (M / (d + tp); Table D3.1 Case 1), plate block shear for the two outer blocks and through one row, beam flange block shear, beam flexure at the bolt holes (Eq. F13-1, when it applies), the compression plate (Sect. J4.4, K = 0.65), and the plate-to-face fillet welds (kds = 1.5). The plan now shows the bolts with the gage, the face-to-first-bolt distance and the spacing dimensioned; the elevation draws the plate to its length. `ws_more.flange_plate_bolted`.
- **Validation: AISC Design Examples v16.0 Example II.B-1, LRFD and ASD, every flange plate value** (flange forces 168 / 112 and 161 / 108 kips; bolts 194 / 130; plate 237 / 157 and 183 / 122; block shear 381 / 254, 300 / 200, beam flange 294 / 197; beam flexure 318 / 211 kip-ft; compression plate Lc / r 8.99, 237 / 157; the weld against the example's required 5.51 / 5.54 sixteenths), within 0.6 percent (`tests/test_flange_plate.py`). The 2022 Specification has no 0.85 Ag cap on Ae for connecting elements (J4.1 read); none is applied.
- **Applied, not ruled (conservative):** the compression plate's unbraced length is the larger of the bolt spacing and the distance from the column face to the first bolt; Example II.B-1 uses the bolt spacing only.
- Not checked and listed on the sheet: slip-critical bolts, the web connection, panel shear in the HSS side walls, the beam and column themselves.

- **Steel Beam screen, loads table: new column "Direction"**: down (x axis), the default, or sideways (y axis). Sideways loads bend the beam about its weak axis (`ws_steel_beam.run` splits them out). For every combination with a sideways load the sheet adds: flexure about the y axis (AISC 360-22 Sect. F6 for I-shapes; Sect. F7 / F8 for HSS and pipe, through `steel_beamcol.weak_axis_flexure`), shear from the sideways loads (Sect. G6, or G4 / G5), and flexure about both axes by Eq. H1-1b with Pr = 0, checked at every station with each x moment against the strength of the unbraced segment it lies in. - Item 4 continued. **Steel Beam screen, loads table: new column "Off the shear center by" (in.)**. For an HSS or pipe on a single simple span, each such load twists the beam by load x distance; both ends are held against twisting and the torque divides between them as a simple-span shear does. New `hss.torsion`: AISC 360-22 Sect. H3.1, Tn = Fcr C (Eq. H3-1), round HSS by Eq. H3-2a / H3-2b with the member length, rectangular by Eq. H3-3 to H3-5 on the larger wall slenderness, C from the shapes table; phi 0.90, Omega 1.67. Rows: torsion (largest Tr against Tc) and, where Tr is over 0.2 Tc, Eq. H3-6 with Pr = 0 scanned along the span (Mrx against the strength of its unbraced segment, Mry from sideways loads, Vr / Vc the larger of the two axes, Tr / Tc). The strength box lists Fcr and Tn; the loads list shows each load's distance from the shear center.
- W shapes, channels, more than one span or an overhang with torsion are refused with the reason. W-shape torsion needs AISC Design Guide 9 (warping); not on file.

H3-6 0.333 / 0.340 where Tr = 0.2 Tc: StruKit 0.335 / 0.337, at the exact point x = 0.70 / 0.71 ft where the example rounds to 0.700 / 0.725 ft). Eq. H3-4 by hand. Opened in a headless browser. ## 2026-11-25 about 15:40 local — 0.214.0 stiffened seated connection (571 tests)
- Zach 15:27: "Keep going yes" (Next items 2 and 3). The two earlier entries of this afternoon carried wrong clock times (15:45 and 16:20); corrected to about 15:15 and 15:25.
- **New screen "Stiffened Seated Connection (welded to the support, bolted to the beam)"** (`stiffened_seat`, mark SS), `ws_more.stiffened_seat_sheet`, side elevation to scale with W, L and lb dimensioned and the reaction at 0.8 W. From the AISC Manual 15th Ed. Part 10 pages on stiffened seated connections and the Table 10-7 / 10-8 discussion (pdf pp. 1167-1170 of the scan, read at 560 dpi): the stiffener carries the whole reaction at 0.8 W from the face; bearing length lb = W less a 1/2 in. setback and 1/4 in. underrun; beam web local yielding and crippling on lb with the least stiffener width for each (Manual Eq. 9-60, 9-63; Table 9-4 constants); the T-shaped weld to the support (both sides of the stiffener for its length, and the seat plate 0.2 L each side) by the elastic method; least support thickness by Manual Eq. 9-2 (doubled when seats are welded on both faces of a web), with the weld strength reduced by the ratio when the support is thinner; least stiffener thickness, the larger of tw Fy,beam / Fy,stiffener and 1.5 w (Fy 50) or 2 w; seat plate 3/8 in. minimum.
- **Validation: AISC Design Examples v16.0 Example II.A-14** (W21x68 on a W14x90 flange, PL 5/8 x 7 x 15, 5/16 in. welds): the elastic-method weld gives 139.4 / 92.9 kips against the Manual Table 10-8 values the example quotes, 139 / 93.0; R1 64.0 / 42.6, R5 75.9 / 50.6 kips; least widths 6.93 / 6.96 and 3.59 / 3.61 in.; least stiffener thickness 0.469 in.; least support thickness 0.238 in. The Manual prints no formula for the T-shaped weld; the elastic method (centroid L / 2.4 from the top, I = 0.25 L^3, stress at the top) is the generally published one and is accepted because it reproduces the table. Opened in a headless browser. Sample: `StruKit-Sample-Calc-Sheet-Stiffened-Seated-Connection.pdf`.
- **Applied, not ruled (conservative):** for stiffener Fy between 36 and 50 ksi the stiffener must be at least 2 w thick (the Manual gives 2 w at 36 ksi and 1.5 w at 50 ksi only).
- Not checked and listed: the top angle, the bolts of the beam to the seat, column web punching (the Manual's simplified limits), the support member.

## 2026-11-25 about 16:50 local — 0.217.0 single angles in bending (585 tests)
- Zach 16:13: "Yes" to Next item 6.
- **New screen "Single-Angle Beam"** (`angle_beam`, mark LB), `strukit/anglebeam.py`, `ws_more.angle_beam_sheet`. Simple span, ends braced; loads uniform (full span) or point, each down or sideways (horizontal, positive from the toe of the horizontal leg toward the heel); orientation by which leg is vertical and whether its toe is up or down; lateral-torsional restraint none, at midspan only, or continuous; own weight as dead load on request.
  - Geometric axis design (AISC 360-22 Sect. F10.2(2)) for an equal-leg angle with down loads only, and for any angle with continuous restraint: yielding 1.5 My (Eq. F10-1); lateral-torsional buckling by Eq. F10-5a (toe in compression) or F10-5b (toe in tension) with My = 0.80 Fy S and no restraint, or 1.25 Mcr and My = Fy S with restraint at the point of largest moment (the screen stops if the largest moment is not at midspan); Cb by Eq. F1-1 for each segment; leg local buckling (Eq. F10-6 to F10-8, Table B4.1b Case 12) with Sc = 0.80 S unless restrained continuously.
  - Principal axis design otherwise (unequal legs without continuous restraint, or any sideways load): moments resolved on the major (w) and minor (z) axes at every station, Eq. F10-4 with Cb (not over 1.5) and beta_w from AISC 360-22 Commentary Table C-F10.1 (positive when the short-leg toe is in compression, negative otherwise), yielding and leg local buckling about both axes, no lateral-torsional buckling about z (User Note), and Eq. H2-1 at the two toes and the heel with each term + where it compresses the point and - where it puts it in tension. Principal-axis properties (Iw, wA..wC, zA..zC) added to the single-angle table from the Shapes Database (tools/parse_angles.py); their signs come from the angle's geometry.
  - Shear of each leg by Sect. G3 (Eq. G3-1, Cv2 with kv = 1.2): the vertical leg for down loads, the horizontal leg for sideways loads.
  - Sheet: loading diagram over the span, the section to scale in its orientation with legs dimensioned, shear and moment diagrams of the down loads.
