- Zach 17:04 / 17:10: can StruKit now run all of 2026-011 with the 3D model and analysis? "Work for an hour and see where you get. Again that job is already fully built so this is just a test." Clock note: the log times of the 0.214.0 to 0.219.0 entries this afternoon were estimates and run up to an hour late; the order is right.
- **Built for it (StruKit, not the job):**
  - Joist and rafter rows that cover only part of the beam they land on: placement box "the row runs on, to its left or right, for" (ft, + to the right of the joist looking from its left end); the load per foot goes only on that length of the carrying beam (or wall).
  - **Bearing walls carry gravity in the model.** A support that lands on a placed wall (shear wall, perforated or force-transfer wall; level = top of the wall) puts its reaction on the wall (a row of joists: per joist x 12 / spacing x the length it covers). A wall stands on the level below its top and passes everything down, spread evenly over its length: onto a wall on the same line below, a beam under it (a uniform load over the wall's extent), or a continuous footing line. A wall with loads and nothing under it is reported; its own weight is not added.
  - **Continuous footings can be placed as a line** (new "other end" boxes); walls and joist rows bear on them as load per foot. A post landing on a footing line is reported (a point load is not spread along a continuous footing).
  - **A footing takes several members at once:** the loads of everything on it are added by load case and then the governing ASD and LRFD combinations are picked (before, a footing took one member and the last one linked won).
  - Members running the same way (two joists end to end) no longer count as carrying each other.
  - Wind to the wall lines: each wind case is taken to its own wall top (the Roof level got no wind when the two wind directions had different wall tops).
  - Sheet header: a long project name no longer runs into the firm name; the site address moves to the second line (found by the overlap audit on this model).
  - Plan view draws footing lines; the 3D view draws them as strips.
- **The 2026-011 model** (`jobs/2026_011_build_model.py`, `tests/data/job_2026_011_model.strukit`, test in `tests/test_model_walls.py`): plan read from sheet S2 by scale (about 0.1 ft): levels Foundation 0, Main floor 9.5, Roof 19 ft; Floor Beams A (three spans on posts and two 3 in. pipe columns), B and C, four joist rows (deck, floor left, right, lower) with their row widths, roof joists wall to wall, three main-level and three lower-level shear walls on their lines, three continuous footing lines and the two pipe column pads; wind and seismic sheets from the lateral project. One press links 20 load paths (joists to beams to posts and columns to footings; roof joists to the Line 1 main wall, down the lower wall to its footing); wind and seismic go to all six walls, stacked, governing load per wall. 3D view shows the whole house. Package: `2026-011-Building-Model-StruKit.pdf` (61 sheets).
- **What StruKit could not represent (gaps, not job findings):** the existing house (Line 2): the lower floor joists and the roof joists end on it, so their loads stop there, and wind across the house has one new wall line, so Line 1 takes all of it; posts sitting on the continuous footings (Column A, Column B, the post under Floor Beam B); wall self-weight and openings in bearing walls; seismic weights from the model; diaphragm sheets from the model; wood members in the 3D frame. Roof Beam A, the deck column and the two headers were left as stand-alone checks (not placed). Added to the Next list as item 8.
- Tests: walls and footing lines by statics (roof row to the wall, wall on wall, floor row, footing per foot), two posts on one footing summed by case before the governing combination, joists end to end, and the 2026-011 model linking and distributing.

## 2026-11-25 18:12 local — 0.224.0 headers in bearing walls (605 tests)
- Zach 18:01: work through the model-gap list (Next item 8) before returning to the main list.
- **A beam placed in a wall line at the wall's top level is a header** (any beam screen: wood, LVL, glulam, Weyerhaeuser, I-joist, steel). In the load path:
  - a row of joists or rafters landing on that wall line is split along the line: the length over each header goes onto the header as a load per foot over the overlap (header's own coordinates), and the wall keeps the rest of the row's length;
  - a wall standing on a wall line with headers: the part of the upper wall over each header goes onto the header as a uniform load (upper wall load / its length) over the overlap; the lower wall takes the share not over headers (shown on the load path line as a percentage);
  - a beam or post landing over the opening lands on the header (beams are searched before walls, as before);
  - the header's end reactions go down its jack studs to what is under the wall at that point: the wall below, a beam, a footing line (spread over the typed length, 0.223.0) or an existing wall; never onto the wall it sits in.
- Placement card help on beam screens says so.
- **Validation by statics** (2026-011 model): a 4 ft header in the upper Line 1 wall: the roof live load reaching the lower wall is unchanged (15,916 lb), the header takes the row over 0 to 4 ft and the wall keeps 22.75 of 26.75 ft; a 5 ft header in the lower wall: the floor live load is conserved between wall and header, the header takes the upper wall over 0 to 5 ft and its ends reach the footing line. ## 2026-11-25 20:12 local — 0.232.0 shear walls in the 3D frame; wood members checked from the frame forces (616 tests)
- Zach 19:50: "You should finish the two things left then rerun the 2026-011 and show me it".
- **Shear walls in the sway run.** Each placed shear wall (shear wall, perforated, force-transfer) is an X-braced panel between its top and bottom ends: rigid end posts, two pinned diagonals sized so the panel's lateral stiffness equals the wall's own (design shear / SDPWS Eq. 4.3-1 deflection of its sheet, segments in parallel; force-transfer walls from their piers over the full height). The wall's top plate runs through every joint on it (beam bearings, column tops, walls standing on it); beams bearing on a wall are held vertically there (the wall carries them down) instead of being fixed; wall bottoms at the lowest level are pinned. With walls in the frame each level is tied by a stiff diaphragm (pinned bars on a Delaunay triangulation of the level's joints). Wind and seismic are shared to the wall tops and column tops by tributary width as before, so the run gives drift, second-order effects and member forces for a wood building. New stiffness boxes on the force-transfer wall screen (end post area, E, anchorage slip). Existing walls are not in the frame (said in the notes).
- **frame3d.py:** a member may set its own number of elements for the second-order run (`nsub`); the fictitious bars use one (a straight two-force bar), which keeps the run fast (2026-011: 64 runs in about 6 s).
- Joints where every member is pinned get their rotation held (the members carry no moment there); column tops bearing on or under wood beams are pinned (a post cap). The sway run's wind takes each wind case to its own wall top (the same fix as 0.220.0's wall lines).
- **Wood members checked from the frame forces** (ASD projects): at every station of every run, NDS Eq. 3.9-1 / 3.9-2 (tension and bending), Eq. 3.9-3 and 3.7 (compression and bending), and shear, with CD from the combination, CF / CV, CL and CP from the member's own screen (bracing, Ke and unbraced lengths; built-up columns with Kf), on the sway table and the printed 3D Frame Analysis sheet ("(wood)" rows with the NDS equation and CD).
- 2026-011 model: the full ASCE/SEI 7-22 ASD and LRFD lists with wind and seismic (Sect. 2.3.1, 2.4.1, 2.3.6, 2.4.5) and end post data on the six walls ((2) 2x6 No. 2 Douglas Fir-Larch, E from Table 4A; slip the STHD14 catalog deflection) as test inputs.
- Cover sheet: a combination's source wraps inside the right margin (the seismic combinations' long sources ran past it; found by the overlap audit on this model).

## 2026-11-25 21:32 local — 0.235.0 base plates linked to their footing, or stand-alone (626 tests)
- Zach 21:24: "The anchor rod calcs need to be able to link to a foundation system or be independent."
- New card on the four base plate screens (Base Plate, Base Plate in Uplift, Biaxial, Base Plate with Moment): "Foundation under this plate (optional)". Blank = stand-alone, exactly as before. Pick an Isolated Footing or a Footing with Moment and, for a footing that is not square, which footing dimension the plate length N runs along.
- Linked: the footing's plan, thickness and f'c replace the plate's concrete boxes (support size, f'c, A2/A1 on the biaxial screen, concrete length, width and thickness at the rods), with the plate centred on the footing; the footing's base plate boxes take the plate's size (column "steel"). - Item 5 of the 01:11 run. New choice on the Seismic Base Shear screen: "Building model: story force to the wall lines": evenly over the building width (blank, as before) or **by the weight in the building model**. By weight, each wall line takes the story force times the fraction of the level's placed seismic weight lying between the half-way lines to its neighbours (the outer lines take everything beyond them): walls by their length (half to each level, as the weight takeoff), beams by their length, joist rows over the rectangle they cover. `model_seismic_weights` now returns where each weight sits (`pieces`); `_weight_in_strip` integrates it. ## 2026-11-26 09:05 local — 0.250.0 deflection of force-transfer shear walls (Next item 4) (657 tests)
- **Force Transfer Around Openings screen:** with the end post area, its E and the anchorage slip typed (the boxes the 3D frame already used, now labelled "Deflection: ..."), the sheet adds a Deflection block: each pier by SDPWS-2021 Eq. 4.3-1 at its own unit shear (the one-opening Diekmann value, or the T555 pier value with several openings) and its own length, once for each direction of load; the wall deflection is the average of the pier values. The published IIBEC example prints only the six pier deflections and their average (0.826 in.), not its E, A, Ga, nail slip or hold-down slip, so it cannot be reproduced; no published example with all inputs was found (SDPWS-2021 and its commentary give no FTAO deflection method).

  M / (d + (tp top + tp bottom) / 2); the bolt or weld pattern to the beam flange is the same on both. Rows are labelled "Top plate:" and
  "Bottom plate:"; the elevation draws both plates at their own thickness and a second plan shows the bottom plate.
- **Validation:** the same plate top and bottom reproduces the single-plate sheet; the bottom plate's face checks equal a single-plate run with
  that plate at the same lever arm; flange forces by hand (`tests/test_beam_hss_moment.py`). The single-plate checks stay validated by Design
  Guide 24 Example 6.3 and Design Examples II.B-1 / II.B-2.

## 2026-11-26 15:00 MST — 0.262.0 stiffened girders: end-panel tension field action, continuous girders, bearing under loads (Next 2, done)
- **End panel with tension field action, AISC 360-22 Sect. G2.3 (Eq. G2-12 to G2-15)**, when tension field action is asked and bearing
  stiffeners are given (they are the end post): de, Mpf (the smaller flange with de of web, Commentary G2.3), Mpst (the bearing stiffener pair
  with de plus the end distance of web, the end distance not over 0.84 tw (E / Fy)^0.5; new box "girder end to the inside face of the bearing
  stiffeners", blank = the stiffener thickness), beta_v, Vn; used for the end panel where larger than Sect. G2.1; the tension flange stress
  alpha Mr / Sxt in the end panel checked against 0.35 Fy (`builtup.end_panel_tfa`).
- **Continuous built-up girders** now take the stiffener and bearing checks: panels are measured from every support into each span (to half
  the span, or the whole overhang), the end panels at every support without tension field action (G2.3 only at the girder ends); interior
  supports by Eq. J10-2 / J10-4, or bearing stiffeners with a 25 tw web strip; reactions from the continuous solution with the live load
  arrangements.
- **Concentrated loads** (new boxes: bearing length of the point loads; bearing stiffeners under them yes / no): web local yielding and crippling
  under each point load (Eq. J10-2 / J10-4, or J10-3 / J10-5 within d of an end), or bearing stiffeners (Eq. J7-1, column with a 25 tw strip,
  12 tw within d of the end). The side view draws the stiffeners at every support and point load.
- **Validation: by hand** (no published example of G2.3 or of these bearing checks is on file): G2.3 on the G.8A girder (Cv2 0.635, de 0.297
  in., Mpf 454, Mpst 103 kip-in., beta_v 1.0, Vn 280.5 kips), the two-span girder (reactions 375 / 127.5 kips with patterned live load, panel
  shear 161.25 kips), point loads (242.2 / 164.4 / 582.4 kips) (`tests/test_builtup.py`).

## 2026-11-26 15:45 MST — 0.263.0 stiffened seats: on an HSS column face; bolted angle leg width-to-thickness (Next 3, done)
- **Stiffened Seated Connection screen, "Support is the face of an HSS column"** (column, face, Fy, Fu and source, column axial load and moment
  at the joint with their source): a welded plate stiffener on one face of a square or rectangular HSS, as AISC Design Guide 24 (2nd Ed.)
  Sect. 5.2.6 describes. The support thickness and Fu are the column's (design thickness), so the Manual Eq. 9-6 base metal check of the
  welds applies to the HSS wall. Added limit states (DG24 Sect. 5.1): face plastification under the stiffener as a longitudinal plate in
  in-plane bending, Mn = Fy t^2 L [1 / (2 eta) + 2 / (1 - beta)^0.5 + eta / (1 - beta)] Qf (DG24 Eq. 2-6, Manual Eq. 9-46), against R x 0.8 W,
  with Qf for a longitudinal plate (Spec Eq. K1-3); wall shear yielding (punching), Manual Eq. 10-10 (Fu t L^2 / 5); the stiffener on the flat
  of the face. Bolted angles, seats on both faces and round HSS are refused. The picture draws the HSS cut lengthwise (walls hatched) and in plan.
- **All-bolted stiffened seats:** the outstanding angle leg W / t is checked against 0.45 (E / Fy)^0.5 (Table B4.1a Case 3).
- **Validation: by hand** (no published example of a stiffened seat on an HSS is on file; DG24 2nd Ed. gives the method in words only):
  Mn 955.2 kip-in., punching 1,215.8 kip-in., Qf with 200 kips in the column, the screen and the sheet (`tests/test_stiffened_seat.py`).
  The rest of the seat stays validated by Example II.A-14 and Table 10-7.

## 2026-11-26 16:45 MST — 0.264.0 single angles over several spans and overhangs, on the Steel Beam screen, and in the 3D frame (Next 1, done)
- **Single-Angle Beam screen: more spans and overhangs** (boxes as on the Steel Beam screen). The down and sideways loads are solved as
  continuous beams (`contbeam`, uniform stiffness), the live load arranged span by span (Sect. 4.3.3) for the down loads; Sect. F10 / H2 at
  every station with each span (or overhang) between supports as an unbraced length, Cb by Eq. F1-1 (not over 1.5 on the principal axes), and
  the negative beta_w over a segment where the major-axis moment reverses (Commentary F10.2). With continuous restraint, the geometric axis
  with each flange in compression in turn. Deflection: both directions solved with EI = 1 and resolved on the principal axes, against span /
  ratio (2 x overhang / ratio). Reactions at every support for the takedown. Midspan restraint, axial load and sideways live loads on a
  continuous angle are refused. The loading picture draws every support and dimensions the spans.
- **Steel Beam screen with a single angle** (for example L4x4x1/4): new boxes for the vertical leg, the toe and the Fy source; the sheet is the
  Single-Angle Beam sheet (bracing: continuous, at the supports only, or at midspan of a single span); reactions for the takedown.
- **3D frame:** an angle placed as a steel beam takes its principal-axis stiffness (Iw, Iz, J) with the member's local axes along the principal
  axes (the horizontal leg toward the member's local y). It is checked on its own sheet, not from the frame forces (noted on the frame sheet).
- **Validation:** statics and Table 3-23 by hand (two-span support moment w L^2 / 8, shear 5 w L / 8, Cb 2.27 from the quarter-point moments,
  overhang span moment 1.218 kip-ft, two-span deflection with live on one span 0.0092 w L^4 / E I); the F10 / H2 sum at the governing station;
  the negative beta_w; the 3D frame against the Commentary F10.2 ratios (1.572 down, 0.949 sideways) (`tests/test_angle_beam.py`).

## 2026-11-26 16:45 MST — 0.267.0 single angles checked from the 3D frame forces (Next 3)
- In the sway run (direct analysis, second order), an angle placed as a steel beam is checked at every station of every run by Eq. H2-1 on
  its principal axes from the frame's own element forces (the element axes are the principal axes, 0.264.0): P / Pc (Sect. E3 / E7 with the
  member length) or P / Pt (yielding) plus Mw / Mcw plus Mz / Mcz, the three terms added (the signs at the toes are not tracked); Mcw and Mcz
  the smaller of the two moment signs, the negative beta_w, Cb = 1.0, Lb from the screen's brace points (else the whole member; continuous
  bracing: no lateral-torsional buckling); shear on the legs (Sect. G3). Compression in an angle with slender legs (Sect. E4 not built) is
  noted and not checked from the frame.

  the ratio equals Mw / Mcw + Mz / Mcz (`tests/test_angle_beam.py`).

## 2026-11-26 17:30 MST — 0.269.0 several openings in one diaphragm (Next 3)
- **Wood Diaphragm screen: new table "More openings"** (a, b, support to near edge, chord to opening, e1, e2), on top of the one-opening boxes.
  Openings side by side across the depth with the same position and length along the span (x0, a, e1, e2) are one region and are solved together
  by the APA T555 several-openings method turned flat (`diaphragm.openings`, `_openings_in_line`): the strips beside and between the openings
  are the piers; strip shear v = (V/W)(d + T)/d, corner zones (vd − F)/d, corner force per foot F/d; members along the opening sides
  |va − vc| × the larger extension; members across the depth from the running force chord to chord. Openings elsewhere along the span are
  separate regions, each with its own steps, check row and enlarged view. Regions that overlap along the span, and openings that touch or
  overlap across the depth, are refused (no published method for them).
- Deflection: the opening term is summed over the regions.
- Picture: every opening in the plan; one enlarged view per region; the chord force labels move beside the line of resistance when no clear
  stretch of chord between the openings' framing is long enough.
- **Validation:** no published diaphragm example is on file. Cross-checked against the T555 shear wall routine (`shearwall.ftao_multi`,
  itself tested against APA's published two-window wall) with the inputs mapped (wall length = W, height = Lr, opening height = a, sheathing
  below = e2): strip shears, corner zones and corner forces agree; Σ v d = V; every shear line nets zero (`tests/test_diaphragm.py`).

- (Zach: "Yes go".) **Stiffened Seat screen, HSS face: new boxes "stiffeners" (one, centred / two) and "two stiffeners, centre to centre".**
  AISC Design Guide 24 (2nd Ed.) Sect. 5.2.6 allows single or double stiffeners but gives no procedure for two; applied (conservative, not
  ruled; in the rulings file):
  - each stiffener takes R / 2 at 0.8 W: face plastification (DG24 Eq. 2-6) and punching (Manual Eq. 10-10) under each, and each
    stiffener's T-shaped weld for R / 2;
  - the pair also checked as one longitudinal plate g + ts wide carrying R (Eq. 2-6 with beta = (g + ts) / B), the yield lines around both;
  - both stiffeners on the flat (g + ts not over B − 4.5 t);
  - the seat plate as a simple span g between the stiffeners under R at mid-span (the beam web), AISC 360-22 Eq. F11-1, plate W wide;
    the beam flange's help is not taken.
- Picture: both stiffeners hidden under the seat plate in the plan, the spacing dimensioned, the seat bolts drawn clear of them.
- **Validation: by hand** (no published example): the Example II.A-14 seat on an HSS16x16x5/8 with stiffeners 6 in. apart
  (`tests/test_stiffened_seat.py`).
